Strong Support Team
GHL India Ventures is a SEBI-registered Category II Alternative Investment Fund focused on a high-conviction strategy: recovering value from stressed real estate assets across India.
Headquartered in Chennai, our leadership brings over 25 years of experience spanning private equity, financial services, and strategic advisory.
Read Our Full Story
Our Approach
Six Pillars of Our Investment Philosophy
We don't just invest capital — we invest conviction.
Value Investing
We seek asymmetric risk-reward opportunities in stressed assets and emerging ventures where intrinsic value is deeply misunderstood.
Risk Management
A multi-layered risk framework ensures downside protection across every allocation, from macro stress testing to deal-level hedging.
Fundamental Analysis
Every investment undergoes 360-degree forensic diligence — financial modelling, management scoring, and sector benchmarking.
Liquidity Management
We engineer liquidity windows and secondary exit pathways to balance portfolio agility with long-term conviction.
Transparency & Accountability
Institutional-grade reporting, quarterly NAV disclosures, and open-door governance that treats every investor as a partner.
Ethical / ESG Considerations
We integrate environmental, social, and governance criteria at every stage, because sustainable returns demand responsible capital.

Frequently Asked Questions
Common Investor Questions
Everything you need to know about investing with GHL India Ventures, SEBI registration, and our fund structure.
What is a Category II AIF?
A Category II Alternative Investment Fund (AIF) is a SEBI-regulated pooled investment vehicle that invests in real estate, private equity, distressed assets, and unlisted companies. GHL India Ventures is registered under SEBI Registration No. IN/AIF2/24-25/1517, with mandatory quarterly audits and third-party custodial safeguards.
What is the minimum investment in GHL India Ventures?
The minimum investment for the Direct AIF Route is as per SEBI regulations for Category II AIFs. For the SEBI Co-Invest Framework, please contact our advisory team for current eligibility and investment details — ideal for salaried professionals looking to access institutional-grade returns.
Is GHL India Ventures SEBI registered?
Yes. GHL India Ventures is registered with the Securities and Exchange Board of India (SEBI) as a Category II Alternative Investment Fund under Registration Number IN/AIF2/24-25/1517. This ensures institutional-grade governance, transparent NAV reporting, and strict regulatory compliance.
How does stressed real estate investment work?
Stressed real estate investment involves acquiring distressed or undervalued properties through NCLT (National Company Law Tribunal) resolutions under the IBC (Insolvency and Bankruptcy Code) at 40-60% discounts below replacement cost. GHL India Ventures revitalizes these assets — completing construction, clearing legal encumbrances, and repositioning for sale or lease.
What is the expected IRR on the fund?
Specific fund performance data is available to registered investors after KYC verification. Our stressed real estate strategy is designed for significant value creation through deep-discount NCLT acquisitions. Past performance is not indicative of future results. Please contact our advisory team at +91 7200 255 252 for detailed information.
Can NRIs invest in GHL India Ventures?
Yes, Non-Resident Indians (NRIs) can invest through their NRO account. GHL India Ventures handles all FEMA/RBI compliance documentation. Our team has successfully onboarded NRI investors from the US, UK, UAE, Singapore, and Australia.

THE FUTURE IS FUNDED, NOT OWNED: 
How real estate fund of AIFs Outperform Traditional Real Estate
In today’s volatile yet opportunity-rich real estate landscape, wealthy investors and institutions 
are shifting away from direct property investments-choosing instead to invest in professionally managed Real Estate Funds under Alternative Investment Funds (AIFs). Why? Because AIFs 
don't just buy property-they unlock hidden value, ﬂip distress, and engineer structured growth.
India’s ﬁnancial ecosystem is ﬂooded with high-value distressed assets and NPAs-prime real 
estate locked in litigation, under-utilization, or ﬁnancial default.Here’s how Real Estate AIFs are outperforming traditional property investments:
1. Flipping Distressed Assets for High Returns
AIFs with experienced teams and legal capabilities can acquire these assets at signiﬁcant 
discounts, resolve issues, redevelop or reposition them, and exit at far higher valuations. This value-ﬂipping strategy offers double-digit IRRs that traditional investors can rarely access or execute alone.
Investor Advantage: Participate in institutional-grade NPA resolution and proﬁt 
from distress-to-value transformation.
Instead of concentrating capital in one property, AIFs invest across multiple real estate projects, 
asset classes, and geographies.
This signiﬁcantly reduces risk while enhancing overall return potential.3. Diversiﬁcation Beyond a Single PropertyUnlike buying a property and relying on brokers or hearsay, AIFs are managed by seasoned 
investment professionals, asset managers, and legal experts.2. Managed by Experts, Not mediators
They employ institutional processes, ﬁnancial modeling, legal due diligence, and portfolio-level 
risk mitigation strategies-ensuring your capital works smarter, not harder.
Investor Advantage: High conﬁdence in governance, execution, and compliance critical for 
NRIs, UHNIs, and family ofﬁces.
Investor Advantage: Exposure to a diversiﬁed real estate portfolio with balanced risk and reward.
Many AIFs offer structured payouts combining ﬁxed income and capital appreciation. Some 
follow a priority waterfall model, where investors get their capital and returns before the fund manager earns performance fees.4. Structured, Predictable Returns
Investor Advantage: Predictability of income with upside potential-ideal for those seeking passive yet high-yield strategies.VENTURESINDIA
Direct real estate ownership involves documentation, maintenance, legal risks, tenant issues, 
and resale efforts.
The old model of buying individual real estate assets is giving way to a smarter alternative: 
professionally managed real estate funds.
By ﬂipping distressed assets, using structured strategies, and leveraging deep market 
intelligence, AIFs deliver superior, risk-adjusted returns-with zero operational stress.With AIFs, investors avoid all operational burdens while still beneﬁting from real estate-linked 
growth.5. No Operational Headaches
Investor Advantage: Pure-play capital appreciation and income-zero involvement in property management.
Real estate AIFs are regulated by SEBI, offering transparency and investor protection.For NRIs, 
UHNIs, family ofﬁces, and institutions, this provides a compliant and tax-efﬁcient route to participate in India’s real estate market.6. Ideal for Global Investors and NRIs
Structured  Asset Owned your futureInvestor Advantage: Regulatory security, simpliﬁed taxation, and repatriation support.VENTURESINDIA

VENTURESINDIA
Our portfolio company successfully acquired 3.75 acres of strategically located commercial land, 
positioned adjacent to the upcoming Media City and directly behind the Mahindra Club House. This premium location offers high visibility and signiﬁcant potential for commercial development, making it a valuable addition to our investment pipeline.
To enhance the asset's market readiness and credibility, approval from the Land Development Authority was promptly sought. This move not only strengthened the land's regulatory proﬁle but also increased its overall value in the eyes of potential buyers.
Following this, the property was sold for /uni20B910 crores, compared to an acquisition cost of /uni20B96 crores, 
resulting in an impressive proﬁt of /uni20B94 crores, marking a 66% return. This successful outcome reﬂects our portfolio company’s ability to identify high-potential opportunities, navigate approval processes effectively, and deliver strong returns through strategic asset management.CASE STUDY: PREVIOUS PROJECT

6|Page   
2. Interest  
The Interest for the Debenture is fixed at 12 % per annum (payable @ 
1%/month). TDS shall be deductible as per the rate specified in Income Tax 
act, 1961. The total sum of interest payable per annum inclusive of TDS is Rs. 
1,44,000/Annum (One Lakh Forty Four Thousand Rupees only)  
 
3. Appreciation  
The Debenture Value shall accrue appreciation at the rate of twelve percent 
(12%) per annum, calculated on a simple interest basis and not on a 
compounding basis. Such appreciation shall be added to the principal 
Debenture Value as premium and at the time of redemption which shall be 
treated as premium payable upon redemption. The applicable appreciation 
rates, accrual methodology, and the corresponding redemption values shall be 
as published and updated from time to time in the Investor Dashboard section 
of the GHL India Ventures website, and such published information shall be 
deemed to form an integral and binding part of this Agreement.  
 
4. Tenure 
Total tenure is 10 years with option to redeem at any time after 3 years at the 
option of the investor. 
 
5. Redemption  
The Debentures will be redeemed at a premium @ 12% per Annum by 
the Company at the end of the tenure. Upon full and final payment 
towards redemption of debentures, the Company shall file a Satisfaction of 
charge with the Registrar of Companies.  
 
6. Security Creation  
The Company shall, for securing the due repayment of the Debentures 
together with interest, appoint a Debenture Trustee in accordance with 
applicable laws. Upon completion of the issue and allotment of the 
Debentures, the Company shall execute a Mortgage Deed – Pledge 
Agreement in respect of the identified asset (Property, Units of AIF) in favour 
of the Debenture Trustee, who shall hold the same for the benefit of and in 
trust for the Debenture-holders.

The Company represents to the Investor that:

10. Dispute Resolution:  
10.1 If any dispute arises between any of the Parties hereto during the 
subsistence or thereafter, in connection with the validity, interpretation, 
implementation or alleged breach of any provision of this Agreement or 
regarding any question, including the question as to whether the 
termination of this Agreement by any Party hereto has been legitimate, the 
Parties hereto shall endeavour to settle such dispute amicably. The attempt 
to bring about an amicable settlement is considered to have failed as soon as 
one of the Parties hereto, after reasonable attempts, which attempt shall 
continue for not less than 15 (fifteen) days, gives 15 (fifteen) days notice 
thereof to the other Party in writing.  
 
10.2 In case of such failure, the dispute shall be referred to a sole arbitrator. 
The arbitration proceedings shall be governed by the provisions of the 
Arbitration and Conciliation Act, 1996. In the event of a failure to agree upon a 
sole arbitrator within a period of 15 (fifteen) days of receipt of notice, 1 (one) 
arbitrator each shall be appointed by the Company, on the one hand, and 
the Investor, on the other hand. The two arbitrators so appointed shall 
together appoint a third arbitrator, who shall be the chairperson of the 
arbitral panel. It is expressly clarified that these dispute resolution provisions 
apply to claims or disputes concerning any and all Parties to this Agreement 
and not just to disputes between the Company and the Investor.  
 
10.3 The arbitration proceedings shall be held at Chennai. The arbitration 
proceedings shall be in English language. The award shall be substantiated 
in writing. The court of arbitration shall also decide on the costs of the 
arbitration proceedings. The award shall be binding on the disputing 
Parties subject to applicable laws and the award shall be enforceable in any 
competent court of law. The provisions of this survive the termination of this 
Agreement for any reason whatsoever. Clause 9 shall The courts at Chennai 
shall have exclusive jurisdiction.  
 
9|Page  11. Governing Jurisdiction:  
This Agreement shall be governed by the laws of the Republic of India. Incase 
dispute araised between the parties to the agreement is not resolved 
through Court of Arbitration, then the parties shall approach the High 
Courts in Chennai, India which has exclusive jurisdiction in respect of all 
disputes arising out of this Agreement.

confidential and not to use, disclose or divulge to any third party or to enable 
or cause any person to become aware of (except for the purposes of the 
Company's business) any confidential information relating to the Company 
including but not limited to IP Rights (whether owned or licensed by the 
Company), lists of customers, reports, notes, memoranda and all other 
documentary records pertaining to the Company or its business affairs, 
finances, suppliers, customers or contractual or other arrangements but 
excluding any information which is in the public domain (otherwise than 
through the wrongful disclosure of any party) or which a party is required to  
disclose by law or by the rules of any regulatory body to it or the Company is 
subject. 
 
12.3 The Company shall bear the costs and disbursements incurred in 
negotiating and closing this Agreement and of matters incidental to this 
Agreement.  
 
12.4 The Company shall not be considered in breach of this Agreement to the

=== SOURCE: Document - GHL India Ventures - Property details.pdf ===
Strategic Land Acquisitions by GHL India Ventures – High -Value Assets with 
Strong Appreciation Potential  
 
1. Narikudi, Madurai – Renewable Energy Development Opportunity  
 
The property, located in Narikudi , approximately 50 kilometres from Madurai, 
Tamil Nadu, spans about 145 acres of contiguous land — making it ideal for large -
scale renewable energy development.  
 
A proposal has been initiated to establish a solar power plant on this land, 
leveraging the region’s abundant sunlight, favourable terrain, and existing 
infrastructure. Importantly, the site is situated close to a major power substation, 
making it highly s uitable for solar power generation and seamless grid 
connectivity.  
 
The prospective buyer, based in Dubai, has shown strong interest in developing 
this project, recognising the region’s growing prominence as a renewable energy 
hub. The nearby SEI Aadhavan Power Plant and SEI Kathiravan Power Plant, both 
operated by the Gre enko Group, further reinforce the area’s renewable 
ecosystem.  
 
The property was acquired at ₹22.5 crores, representing a 40% discount on the 
current market value. After initial development and site preparation, the total 
investment is projected at ₹25 crores.  
With its strategic location, ample land area, and proximity to the power 
substation and existing solar infrastructure, the asset stands as a well -chosen 
investment with excellent potential for high -value appreciation.  
 
 
 
 
2. Karadivavi, Coimbatore – Industrial and Strategic Development Asset  
 
Located in Karadivavi, around 35 kilometres from Coimbatore, Tamil Nadu, this 
30-acre land parcel offers prime potential for industrial and infrastructure 
expansion.  
 
The property lies near the Defence Corridor and in proximity to the Sulur 
Airbase, adding significant strategic and commercial value. The surrounding area 
is rapidly evolving into a major industrial and logistics hub, supported by growing 
defence and manufacturing investments.  
 
The acquisition was made at ₹45 crores, reflecting a 40% discount on prevailing 
market rates. Post -development and value enhancement, the property is 
expected to achieve a fair market value of approximately ₹75 crores.  
 
Given its location advantage, connectivity, and industrial potential, this 
acquisition demonstrates strong investment foresight and offers excellent 
prospects for capital appreciation and diversified project development.  
 
Summary:  
The acquisitions at Narikudi (Madurai) and Karadivavi (Coimbatore) were made 
by GHL India Ventures at approximately 40% below market value. Both 
properties reflect strategic selection, inherent growth potential, and synergy 
with GHL’s core investment focus  in renewable energy and industrial expansion 
sectors.

 SECTION II: MARKET OPPORTUNITY / INDIAN ECONOMY / INDUSTRY 
OUTLOOK  
 
The information in this section, unless otherwise stated, (1) represents the research results, market views 
or opinions of the Investm ent Manager based on available information and current market conditions 
and (2) is provided as on the date hereof. Information obtained from third parties and other sources have 
not been independently verified by the Investment Manager. Growth and other n umerical data shown 
does not represent investment returns and should not be used to predict the Scheme ’s return.  
 
Indian Economy  
India is expected to be one of the fastest growing economies in 2023. After rapid economic growth of 
7.2% in the FY2023, economic momentum has remained strong in the first quarter of FY2024, with GDP 
growth of 7.8% year -on-year (YoY). The strong growth rat e was despite high base year effect of 
Q1FY2023. In the view of the Investment Manager, all recent economic indicators for India signal towards 
expansionary economic conditions in the near future, reinforced by strong revival in domestic demand.  
The stress  on India’s public finance has gradually reduced since the opening of the economy as GST 
monthly collections remains on target, moderation of crude oil prices, stable FII inflows and control on 
expenditure growth. Barring another external shock in the form  of the impending global recession across 
Europe and USA, the government’s fiscal consolidation path looks achievable.  
India’s current account deficit (CAD) had been declining in the past few years due to global trade 
restrictions during the pandemic. Wit h the opening of the economy, imports surged due to  higher 
commodity prices (oil, gold). This led to widening of trade deficit and hence increased CAD. Net invisible 
receipts were higher in 2022 -23 due to an increase in net exports of services and net priv ate transfer 
receipts. The sustained large FDI inflows over the past decade have helped to reduce India's external 
account vulnerability and have contributed to boost India's foreign exchange reserves.  
India's external debt as a percentage of GDP is gettin g lower than 20% and is better positioned than most 
of the low and middle -income countries (LMICs) as measured by select vulnerability indicators, such as 
share of short -term debt in total external debt, external debt to GNI (Gross National Income), forex 
reserves to external debt and external debt to exports. The Investment Manager believes that the less than 
proportionate rise in external debt given the widening trade deficit is an encouraging sign of manageable 
risks in India’s external sector.  
The matur ity profile of India's external debt is dominated by long tenure borrowings. Short term debts are 
approximately 20 per cent of the total external debt and it continuous to remain within manageable levels. 
India’s foreign exchange reserves are at a comforta ble level and more than 70 per cent of the total external 
debt is covered through build up in foreign exchange reserves.  
Recent development in India startup eco -system  
Indian start -up ecosystem is at an inflection point currently. Despite the Covid -19 impa ct, the India startup 
ecosystem has demonstrated robust growth and great resilience. Indian startup ecosystem has registered 
more than 600 transactions with investors from across the globe exhibiting their confidence with USD 11 
billion investments in the first half of 2021. During this period, 16 Indian start -ups entered the coveted 
unicorn club including Digit Insurance, InnovAccer, Cred, Meesho, Gupshup, Pharmeasy, Groww, Urban 
 
17 
 Company, Mohalla Tech (ShareChat and Moj), Chargebee, Moglix, Infra.Market, Z eta, Five Star Business 
Finance, BrowserStack and logistics firm BlackBuck, the latest entrants.  
What is even more heartening is the maturity of the public market. Within the first half of July 2021, three 
large Indian technology players submitted their pr ospectus to go public – Zomato, Paytm and Mobikwik. 
We shall see a number of businesses to tap the public markets going forward after the stellar entry of 
Zomato and expected great IPOs of Paytm and Mobikwik. It is the most opportune time to invest in the 
market.

 SECTION III: INVESTMENT OBJECTIVE, STRATEGY AND PROCESS  
 
Investments  in Alternative  Investment  Funds  are fraught  with the risks  associated  with such 
investments.  The Investment  Manager  has considered  the general  risk factors  and believes  that the 
Trust’s/Investment  Schemes’ investment strategy, objective/purpose, investment 
process/methodology, investment model  and policies as outlined in this Section II and at other 
relevant places in this Master Memorandum will  assist  in addressing  and managing  some of the risks  
involved.  For further  details  on the risk factors,  see “SECTION  VII: RISK  FACTORS  AND  
POTENTIAL  CONFLICTS  OF INTEREST ” in this Master  Memorandum.  
Investment  Objective  
 
The Fund is set up with the objective and purpose to carry  on the activity of a Category I I AIF. The 
investments from the Contribution Fund shall be arranged, made, managed and  disposed of with the 
view to providing long -term returns to the Contributors in accordance with  Applicable  Laws  and the 
Trust  Documents.

Investment  Strategy,  Process  and Methodology  
 
 
19 
 Investment Strategy  
As Investment Managers, our task is to identify the right companies/founders and support them in 
whatever way possible while being acutely aware of how some of the large megatrends are impacting 
business on a daily basis.  Thus, while we will we se ek to continuously evolve with the markets, some 
basic characteristics that all of our portfolio investments will reflect include:  
 
1. Founders who understand consumer behavior, can evolve with the times, and think ahead  
2. A fundamental understanding of the end  market and its needs  
3. Large, organic markets with large enough profit pools  
4. Efficient, logical execution  
5. Presence of differentiators in terms of product/brand/service, process, execution or technology  
 
The intent through our first fund is investing in equity/equity linked instruments of Companies at the pre -
series A, Series A, pre -series Series B, or Series B stages and doubling down on investments at a later 
stage where appropriate. It will also be our endeavor to try and get minority allocations in sl ightly more 
mature companies with a clear path to profitability. If considered prudent and expedient to take advantage 
of certain investment opportunities, the Investment Manager may also choose to invest in listed equity 
securities (at any time, not excee ding beyond 49% of the overall fund for publicly listed securities).  
Investment Process  and methodology  
The Investment Manager follows a very disciplined and conscious approach to investing. All investments 
will go through a detailed round of financial, co mmercial and legal due diligence; in -depth background 
and reference checks of founders, and a clear understanding on where the investment team can add value. 
The process is outlined below:  
 
Deal sourcing : This will be done though the Investment Manager’s extensive network, organic sources, 
portfolio companies’ network, partnership with angel networks who want their portfolio companies to get 
to the next level, soliciting applications on website, and active scanning by the team of the Investment 
Manager for  any identified sectors, referr als from portfolio companies, boutique investment banks etc.  
 
Screening:  Here the companies are evaluated based on multiple quantitative and qualitative criteria to 
generate a score/view that will help decide whether the Inve stment Manager wishes to spend more time 
on the opportunity.  
 
Detailed Evaluation:  For those that make it through the screening stage, the Investment Manager would 
evaluate the full financial model to understand revenue and profit trajectory. This would b e combined 
with an analysis of industry trends and key competitor data. The Investment Manager shall hold multiple 
meetings with the founders and their team, and also verify and do a reference check on background 
through various sources. The Investment Man ager shall evaluate whether their growth and profitability 
estimates align with their strategy and the potential of the market they operate in. Relevant operational 
experts will likely be brought in as well to provide a t hird-party expert view.  
 
Term shee t: At this stage the Investment Manager would make an offer and negotiate a term sheet for the 
investment.  
 
 
20 
 Due diligence : Financial, commercial and legal due diligence through external auditors, lawyers and 
consultant may follow.  
 
Documentation and clos ing: Detailed transaction documents will be drawn up to reflect the terms agreed 
upon and after the fulfilment of conditions precedent the deal closing would take place.  
 
Strategic Support:  While the Fund shall be invested in the company, the Investment Ma nager shall provide 
the company access to its network and act as its advisors and sounding board. The Investment Manager 
shall leverage its resources and relationships to provide all the necessary inputs that it has access to for 
the success of the Portfol io Companies.  
 
Exit:  At an appropriate time as the investment matures, the Fund will generate returns through exits from 
its Portfolio Companies which would include (partial or full) strategic sale, IPO, sale to a financial 
investor, redemption, etc.  
  
  
 
21 
 Supplementary  Section  
 
 
Investment  Restrictions  
 
The Fund shall make investments subject to the following restrictions, as well as others as required by 
SEBI:  
(i) The Fund shall invest ‘ primarily ’ in the unlisted securities of Portfolio Entities.  
 
(ii) The Fund shall not invest more than 25% (twenty -five percent) of its Investable Funds in one 
Portfolio Entity.  
 
(iii) The Fund shall not borrow funds directly or indirectly or engage in leverage except for meeting 
temporary funding requirements for not more tha n 30 (thirty) days, not more than 4 (four) 
occasions in a year and not more than 10% (ten percent) of the Investable Funds . 
 
(iv) The Fund shall not invest in Associates, except with the approval of Super -Majority of 
Contributors.  
 
(v) The Fund does not propose to engage in lending activity or extending guarantee to Portfolio 
Entities.  
 
(vi) The Fund does not intend to invest in units of other AIFs.

Change  of the Investment  Manager  
The Investment Manager may be changed for a “Cause” (as described below) if 90% (Ninety 
Percent)  of Contributors by value in the Trust vote to change the Investment Manager after giving 
a 6 (Six)  months  written  notice  (“Cure  Period ”) to the Investment  Manager  of such “Cause”  and 
the Investment  Manager  fails to cure such “Cause”  within  the Cure  Period.  
Change  for “Cause”  
 
For the purposes of change of the Investment Manager, “Cause” shall be established where of the  
following circumstances is proven before the highest c ourt of appeal, and causes the 
Trust/Investment  Schemes  to suffer  a material  financial  disadvantage:  
(i) the Investment Manager is in material breach of its material obligations under the terms of 
 
36 
 the Material Documents for which a written notice has been served upon the Investment 
Manager  (through the Trustee) specifying the nature of breach and the Investment Manager 
has failed to  cure such default within a period of 30 (Thirty) days from the date of receipt 
of such notice by  the Investment  Manager;  
(ii) proce eding  of bankruptcy,  insolvency,  administration,  involuntary  reorganization  or 
similar  proceedings  have been admitted  against  the Investment  Manager;  or 
 
(iii) the Investment  Manager  has engaged  in Malfeasance  in connection  with its role as the 
Investment  Manager.  
 
In such an event (if the “Cause” has not been cured), the Super -Majority of Contributors of the 
Trust  may appoint  another  investment  manager.  
Notwithstanding anything stated under this Master Memorandum, upon the removal of the 
Investment  Manager, the Management Fee till the date such removal of Investment Manager is 
effected, shall be  due and payable  to the Investment  Manager.  No conditions  are envisaged  where  the 
Investment  Manager  shall be ineligible  to receive  the Management  Fee. 
       For more details, kindly refer to “SECTION X: RISK FACTORS ” of this Memorandum.

SECTION VII: PRINCIPAL TERMS OF THE SCHEME  
 
This is a description of the principal terms (“ Principal Terms of the  Scheme ”) pertaining to  Water Mount 
Fund . This section provides the Principal Terms of the Scheme , which may be provided in detail elsewhere 
in this Memorandum . The terms hereof are subject to modification or withdrawal prior to First Closing 
and/or Subsequent Closings.  
 
1.  SIZE OF THE 
SCHEME  The target Corpus of the Scheme is up to Rs. 50,00,00,000  (Indian  
Rupees  Fifty  Crores)  with a green  shoe option  of up to Rs. 50,00,00,000  
(Indian  Rupees  Fifty  Crores).   
 
The Investment Manager may, in its discretion, increase or decrease the 
size of the Scheme in compliance with the Regulations.  
2.  TARGET 
INVESTORS  Target Investors of the Scheme are institutional investors, high net worth 
individuals, feeder funds, corporates, financial institutions, banks, 
insurance companies, foreign investors, and other permissible investors.  
 
Eligibility Criteria : For the purposes of subscribing to the Units of the 
Scheme, a Person must fulfil the Eligible Person criteria. Further, please 
refer to paragraph 3 on ‘ Classes of Units ’ and paragraph 5 on ‘ Minimum 
Capital Commitment ’ under this “ SECTION VII: PRI NCIPAL 
TERMS OF THE SCHEME ”. 
 
3.  CLASSES OF UNITS  The Scheme shall issue the following Classes of Units:  
 
Class  Description  
Class A Units  To Contributors, who are investing in the Fund 
through a Placement Agent, in accordance with the 
terms of their respective Contribution Agreements, 
the face value of which shall be INR 1,000 (Indian 
Rupees One Thousand). Class A Units shall be 
issued in various Subclasses, for instance Class A1 
Units, Class A2 Units, etc. to signify an interest in 
each Investment Sc heme.  
Class B Units  To the Sponsors and/or such Eligible Person/s who 
are strategically important/valuable to the Trust, as 
the Investment Manager may designate, in 
accordance with the terms of their respective 
Contribution Agreements, the face value of which 
shall be INR 1,000 (Indian  Rupees One Thousand). 
Class B Units shall be issued in various Subclasses, 
 
40 
 for instance Class B1 Units, Class B2 Units, etc. to 
signify an interest in each Investment Scheme.  
Class C Units  To the partners/employees of the Investment 
Manager  and/or employee welfare trust and/or to 
any such  Eligible Persons who are strategically  
important/valuable to the Trust, as the Investment  
Manager  may designate,  the face value  of which  
shall be INR 1,000 (Indian Rupees One Thousand). 
Class C  Units shall be issued in various Subclasses, 
for instance  Class  C1 Units,  Class  C2 Units,  etc. to 
signify  an interest  in each Investment  Scheme.  
Class D Units  To Contributors, who are investing in the Fund (i) 
directly, or (ii) through a SEBI registered 
intermediary which is separately charging such 
Contributor any fee (such as advisory fee or 
portfolio management fee), in accordance with the 
terms of their respective Contribution Agreements, 
the face value of which shall be INR 1,000 (Indian 
Rupees One Thousand). Class D Units shall be 
issued in various Subclasses, for instance Class D1 
Units, Class D2 Units, etc. to signify an interest in 
each Investment Scheme.  
 
 
The Scheme  shall issue Units in accordance with the Trust Documents. 
It is clar ified that Units may include a fraction of a Unit evidencing 
Beneficial Interest in the Scheme  of a value less than the face value of 
the respective class of Units.  It is clarified that currently no investors 
are expected to invest through Placement Agent /s or SEBI -registered 
intermediaries which is separately charging such Contributor any fee 
(such as advisory fee or portfolio management fee) . In case such 
investors  are onboard ed, then new Classes of Units shall be issued to 
such investors.

discretion issue additional Class/es or Subclass/es/series of Units from 
time to time, which may inter alia  have differential rights, variable 
fee/charge structure, face value,  currency den omination etc. The 
Investment Manager shall have the right to designate/re -designate any 
Class/Subclass to another Class/Subclass. All existing and future 
Class/es and/or Subclass/es of Units shall participate in the common 
investment portfolio of the Sche me except in case of a Defaulting 
Contributor.

 companies/entities/affiliates/strategic partners, make an additional

7.  CO INVESTMENT 
OPPORTUNITIES  Subject to and in compliance with the Regulations and Applicable Laws 
(including applying for any additional license/s as may be required 
under the SEBI (Portfolio Managers) Regulations, 2020), the 
Investment Manager may, at its discretion, offer Co -investment 
opportunities to certain eligible Contributors (in accordance with 
certain qualification criteria which,  inter alia, may be a minimum 
threshold of Capital Commitment by the Contributors or the strategic 
value that a Co -Investor may add to the Portfolio Entity and/or the 
Trust) (each a “Co -Investor”), at such times and on such terms as it may 
determine.  
The t erms on which Co -Investors participate in investments alongside 
the Trust may be similar to the terms on which the Trust participates in 
those investments, or they may be on different terms in accordance with 
Applicable Laws. Co -investment in a Portfolio E ntity by the Investment 
Manager and/or Sponsors and/or a Co -Investor shall never be on terms 
more favourable than those offered to the Trust. Further, the terms of 
exit from the Co -Investment in a Portfolio Entity including timing of 
exit shall be identica l to the terms applicable to that of exit of Trust. The 
Investment Manager may receive any compensation from the Co -
Investors in relation to their Co -investment which could be different 
from the fees or compensation charged to the Trust and shall not be 
obliged to remit these amounts to the Trust. Similarly, any transactional 
expenses in respect of a Co -investment opportunity incurred by the 
Trust shall be shared proportionately between the Trust and such Co -
Investors in the ratio of their amount of investm ents. 
Subject to the foregoing, (i) each of the Trust and the Co -Investor will 
act independently and not as an agent of the other; (ii) each of the Trust 
and the Co -Investor will make its own decisions on investments and 
bear its own expenses as well as be  entitled to the profits and losses 
arising from its investments; and (iii) the Trust and the Co -Investor are 
not expected to act jointly or make any joint decisions or association of 
persons and do not intend to form any joint venture or partnership for 
the purpose of making investment . 
 
44 
 8.  CONTINUING 
INTEREST  The Sponsors will commit an amount equivalent to 2.5% (two and a 
half percent) of the Corpus or Rs. 50,00,000 (Indian Rupees Fifty 
Lakhs), whichever is lower, in aggregate across all the Investment 
Schemes of the Trust, to maintain a continuing interest in accordance 
with the Regulations.  
9.  TERM OF THE 
SCHEME AND 
TERMINATION  Term : The term of the Scheme  shall be a period of 10 (Ten) years  from  
the First Closing which may be extended by the Investment Manager 
by up to 2 (two) years with the prior consent of Two -Third Majority of 
Contributors obtained in accordance with the terms contained in the 
Contribution Agreements and Applicable Laws (“ Term ”). 
 
Termination:  The Trust shall terminate in accordance with the terms 
of the Indenture and Applicable Laws.  
 
Without prejudice to the above, the Scheme  shall terminate on expiry 
of the Term of the Scheme / Trust (as applicable).  
 
Without prejudice to the above, the Investment Manager, may subject 
to the restrictions contained in the Applicable Laws, at any time before 
the expiry of the Term, terminate the Scheme : 
 
 upon a determination by the Investment Manager that: (a) all 
assets held, acquired or agreed to be acquired by the Scheme have 
been sold or disposed of or distributed in specie in accordance 
with the Applicable Law and Trust Documents; or (b) assets 
agreed to be acquired in accordance with the Trust Documents are 
not capab le of being acquired, as the case may be, and related 
Distribution Proceeds or Capital Contributions, in case of 
unconsummated transactions, have been distributed in 
accordance with the Trust Documents and no further drawdowns 
for new investments can be ma de from Contributors;  
 
 if the Trustee (in consultation with the Investment Manager) 
determines that the Scheme  be wound up in the interest of the 
Contributors; or  
 
 if Super -Majority of the Contributors in the Scheme  pass a 
resolution at a meeting of Contr ibutors that the Scheme  be wound 
up; or  
 
 if SEBI so directs in the interest of the Contributors.  
 
 
45 
 The Trustee, through the Investment Manager, shall intimate SEBI and 
the Contributors of the circumstances leading to the winding up of the 
Scheme .  
 
Notwithstanding the termination of the Scheme , the Contributors shall 
continue to remain liable to the following extent:  
 
 The Scheme  will continue for such period of time as may be 
necessary to liquidate existing Scheme  Investments in an orderly 
manner (subject to a maximum period of 1 (one) year from the 
aforesaid date of intimation to the Contributors)  (“Liquidation 
Period ”); 
 
 Capital Commitments will not be extinguished to the extent 
necessary to pay the Scheme Expenses including any Taxes 
payable under Applicable Laws; and  
 
 The Management Fee, Trusteeship Fees and other fees/costs will 
continue to be payable as per provisions of the Trust Documents 
until the Scheme  terminates and is dissolved.  
 
Once the Scheme  liquidates, the proceeds accruing to the Contributors 
shall be distributed to them after satisfying all liabilities of the Scheme  
in accordance with paragraph 25 titled ‘Distributions ’ under this 
“SECTION VII: PRINCIPAL TERMS OF THE SCHEME ”. 
 
To achieve eff ective winding up of the Trust/ Scheme , the Investment 
Manager shall:  
 
 take all practical steps to sell all the non -cash assets of the 
Scheme /Trust;  
 commence arrangements to pay all the liabilities of the 
Trust/ Scheme  including but not limited to the Scheme  Expenses;  
 return to the extent of the available cash in the Contribution Fund, 
all outstanding interests in the Scheme /Trust in accordance with the 
terms contained in the Trust Documents; and  
 take any other action as may be provided under the Indenture.  
 
If there are any unliquidated investments of the Scheme , the Investment 
Manager may, (i) subject to obtaining approval from Super -Majority of 
Contributors, transfer any unliquidated investments to a “Liquidation 
Scheme” in accordance with the Regulations, or (ii) subject to obtaining 
approval from Super -Majority of Contributors, make distributions in 
specie of the Scheme Investments  which may not have been liquidated 
 
46 
 at the end of the Term to the Contributors, in accordance with the terms 
set out under para graph 26 on ‘Distribution in -kind’ under “ SECTION 
VII: PRINCIPAL TERMS OF THE SCHEME ”, or (iii) failing to 
liquidate any unliquidated investments, and failing to receive the 
requisite Contributor consent to carry out the options under sub -clauses 
(i) or (i i) above prior to the expiry of the Liquidation Period, carry out 
mandatory in specie distributions of the unliquidated investments in 
accordance with the Regulations without requiring a Super -Majority of 
Contributors (or write off such unliquidated invest ments in case of any 
Contributor not willing to take the in specie distribution of unliquidated 
investments).  
10.  COMMITMENT 
PERIOD  The Commitment Period for each Contributor shall commence from the 
date of execution of the Contribution Agreement and end on the 
completion of 3 years  from the date thereof. At the end of the 
Commitment Period, the Contributor shall be released from the 
obligation of making any further Capital Contribution against his 
undrawn Capital Commitment (not consisting of amount, towards 
which Drawdown Notice has been issued but payment not received 
from the Contributor and therefore the Contributor shall continue to be 
liable to pay the same even post the Commitment Period) except: (i) to 
the extent necessary to complete Investments, in which a Contributor 
participates, in progress as of the end of the Commitment Period;  
(ii) to the extent necessary to cover the on going expenses and liabilities 
of the Trust/Investment Schemes, including, but not limited to any 
expenses/fees, Tax liabilities and any indemnification obligations; and 
(iii) to create reserves for the above items as deemed appropriate by the 
Investment M anager.  
11.  WAREHOUSED 
INVESTMENTS  
 
 
 
 
 
 In the event the Investment Manager identifies any investment 
opportunities with respect to potential Portfolio Entities and decides to 
make an investment in such Portfolio Entities (“ Warehoused 
Investments ”), the Investment Manager may either itself and/or 
through such Persons identified by the Investment Manager or through 
the Sponsor (“ Warehousing Entity/Individual ”), make investments in 
such Portfolio Entities.  
 
Warehoused Investments may be made at any point pr ior to Final 
Closing.  
 
The transfer of the Warehoused Investments from the Warehousing 
Entity/ Individual to the Scheme  shall be made within 6 (Six) months 
from the date of relevant Closing prior to which such Warehoused 
Investment is made. In the event a  Warehoused Investment is 
 
47 
 transferred to the Scheme  after a period of 6 (Six) months from the date 
of warehousing, the transfer of Warehoused Investment shall be made 
at the fair market value, as reasonably determined by the Investment 
Manager. Further, th e Scheme  shall reimburse the Warehousing 
Entity/Individual for reasonable costs/expenses, but not more than the 
actual costs/expenses, incurred by the Warehousing Entity/Individual . 
 
Prior to the signing of the Contribution Agreement, the Contributor will 
be made aware of the size and sector/ business of the Warehoused 
Investment and the name of the investee entity will be disclosed post 
signing of the Contribution Agreement. In case a Warehoused 
Investment is made post signing of the Contribution Agreement , then 
the information regarding such Warehoused Investment shall be 
disclosed to the Contributor within 15 (fifteen) days from the date of 
such Warehoused Investment.  
 
Refer to risk factor ‘ Associate Party Transactions ’ under paragraph C 
(1) titled “ Risks associated with the nature of the portfolio investments ” 
in “SECTION X: RISK FACTORS ”. 
 
12.  CLOSINGS  The first closing shall be held on or before the expiry of 12 (twelve) 
months from the date on which SEBI takes this Master Memorandum 
on record (“First Closing”), provided that, the Trust has a Corpus of at 
least Rs. 20,00,00,000 (Indian Rupees Twenty  Crores only). 
Investments in Portfolio Entities will be made only after the First 
Closing.  
The Investment Manager has the discretion to hold Subsequent 
Closing(s).  
The final closing means the last of the Closings permitted by the 
Investment Manager for ac cepting Capital Commitments in accordance 
with the terms of the Material Documents (“Final Closing”). The Final 
Closing shall be held not later than 12 (Twelve) months from the First 
Closing. It is clarified that any soft Capital Commitment letter/s or suc h 
other document (as may be agreed between Contributor and Investment 
Manager) executed by the Contributor prior to the date of Final Closing 
shall form part of the Final Closing.  
13.  UNITHOLDERS 
PARTICIPATING IN 
SUBSEQUENT 
CLOSINGS  Contributors admitted to the Scheme (or increasing their Capital 
Commitments to the Scheme) after the First Closing will, upon 
admission (or increase), be required to contribute to the Scheme, as 
applicable: (i) the aggregate amount (other than towards the 
Management Fees) each wo uld have had to contribute to the Scheme 
through the Subsequent Closing had such Contributor been admitted to 
the Scheme at the First Closing (the “ Catch -up Contribution ”), plus 
(ii) an additional amount, as may be determined by the Investment 
 
48 
 Manager (the  “Compensatory Contribution ”) (compounded 
annually, calculated from the date of First Closing till the date of the 
admission (or increase), on such Catch -up Contribution), (iii) 
Management Fees payable retrospectively from the First Closing in 
respect of t heir Capital Commitments to the Scheme; (iv) an additional 
contribution as determined by the Investment Manager (“ Management 
Fees Additional Contribution ”) in respect of the Management Fees 
payable retrospectively from the First Closing in respect of their  Capital 
Commitments to the Scheme, less; (v) their proportionate share of all 
distributions (if any) made to Contributors admitted in prior Closings.   
 
The Investment Manager shall however have the power to waive or 
increase or reduce, on a case -to-case basis, the Compensatory 
Contribution and the Management Fees Additional Contribution.  
  
Any amount paid as Management Fees Additional Contribution will be  
paid to the Investment Manager.  
 
Catch -up Contributions pursuant to the Capital Commitment received 
by the Scheme in such Subsequent Closing or Final Closing may be 
retained as part of the Contribution Fund to be utilized towards Scheme 
Investments or sat isfaction of Scheme Expenses (including towards any 
reserves in that regard), provided any amount not so retained shall be 
immediately distributed (subject to withholding Taxes, as applicable) 
amongst all the Contributors in proportion to their respective Capital 
Contribution and such amounts distributed to the Contributors shall be 
subject to recall by the Scheme.  
 
The Compensatory Contribution applicable to such incoming 
Contributor (i) will not be applied to the subscription of Units in respect 
of such C ontributor; (ii) will not be treated as a Capital Contribution to 
the Scheme in respect of such Contributor; and (iii) will not reduce such 
Contributor’s Capital Commitment.  
 
The Compensatory Contribution collected in connection with a 
Subsequent Closing o r Final Closing shall be distributed (subject to 
withholding Taxes, as may be applicable) amongst the Contributors of 
the Scheme admitted prior to such Subsequent Closing or Final Closing 
(as applicable) requiring the payment of such Compensatory 
Contribut ion, which distribution may be effected either by a pro rata 
refund or set off against their respective undrawn Capital Commitments  
It is clarified that any Tax liability on the payment of Compensatory 
Contribution shall be borne solely by the relevant Con tributors.  
 
 
49 
 It is clarified that any Tax liability on the payment of Compensatory 
Contribution shall be borne solely by the relevant Contributors.  
 
14.  DRAWDOWNS  
 
 
 Contributors will have to make the contributions on an “ as needed ” 
basis  during the Commitment Period depending on the availability of 
investment  opportunities  as determined  by the Investment  Manager.  
 
All investments in Portfolio Entities will be made only after the First 
Closing.  
 
Notwithstanding the above, the Investment Manager may ca ll for such 
portion of the Capital Commitment or the entire Capital Commitment 
at the time of signing of the Contribution Agreement (“Upfront 
Drawdown”) as may be specified under the Contribution Agreement. 
Prior to the utilization of such Upfront Drawdown (s) in accordance with 
the Material Documents, such Upfront Drawdown(s) may be invested 
in Temporary Investments, and the proceeds thereon shall be distributed 
to the Contributors pro -rata to the amount of Capital Contributions 
utilized towards such Tempor ary Investments or utilized and/or 
distributed in accordance with the paragraph on “Temporary 
Deployment of Surplus Funds ” under this “ SECTION VII: 
PRINCIPAL TERMS  OF THE SCHEME ”. 
 
The Drawdown Notice may be sent through facsimile, electronic mail, 
registe red post/courier at the address as may be specified by the 
Contributor in the Contribution Agreement and such Drawdown Notice 
shall be deemed to have been received by the Contributor within 4 
(four) days from the date of dispatch of the registered post/cou rier; 
within 24 (twenty -four) hours from the electronic mail being sent or 
upon receiving the confirmation of transmission of the facsimile.  
The Contributors  shall be required  to make  their Capital  Contribution  
against  their Capital Commitment within 14 (fourteen) calendar days 
from the date  of the Drawdown  Notice.  
 
Amounts  received  from  the Contributors  towards  their Capital  
Commitment  will be treated by the Trust/Investment Schemes as 
consideration for the  issuance  of Units  to the Contributors.  All 
Drawdowns  will be made  in Indian  Rupees  pursuant  to Drawdown  
Notices.  
15.  EXCUSE AND 
EXCLUSION  The Investment Manager may excuse any Contributor from making (or 
utilizing such Excused Contributor’s) Capital Contribution towards any 
proposed Fund Investment by the Fund if (i) the Contributor, based on 
the opinion of a legal professional/legal advisor , confirms that its 
 
50 
 participation in a Fund Investment would violate any Applicable Law; 
and/or (ii) the participation of the Contributor in the Fund Investment 
would be in contravention of the internal policy of the Contributor and 
such Contributor has in formed the Investment Manager in writing of its 
inability or limitation to participate in certain types of investments by 
the Fund at the time of execution of the Contribution Agreement ; and/or 
(iii) the Investment Manager is satisfied that the participati on of any 
Contributor in the Fund Investment would lead to the Fund being in 
violation of any Applicable Law or regulation or would result in 
material adverse effect on the Fund; and/or (iv) the Contributor is an 
alternative investment fund or an investment vehicle, such Contributor 
may be partially excused or excluded from participation in the Fund 
Investment, to the extent of the Capital Contribution of the said 
alternative inves tment fund’s underlying investors who are to be 
excused or excluded from such Fund Investment (“Excused 
Contributor ”). 
 
Any changes to the disclosed internal policy of the Contributor as 
mentioned in point (ii) above shall be intimated to the Investment 
Manager within 15 (fifteen) calendar days of such change.  For excusal 
of any Contributor pursuant to points (iii) and (iv) above, the 
Investment Manager shall record the rationale for such exclusion along 
with the relevant supporting documents.  
 
In the event  that one or more Contributors are excused from 
participation in an investment, the Investment Manager may, either 
elect to have the Fund not make the investment or elect to have the Fund 
make the investment without the participation of such Excused 
Contri butor. In the event the Excused Contributor exercises to not 
participate with respect to any Portfolio Investment in accordance with 
its Contribution Agreement, such Contributor shall have zero allocation 
in such Portfolio Investment (or the portion of the  Portfolio Investment 
in which it does not participate) and will not be entitled to receive any 
distribution of proceeds in respect of such Portfolio Investment (or the 
relevant portion of the Portfolio Investment). Accordingly, the 
allocation of such Excu sed Contributor in other Portfolio Investments 
will correspondingly increase. It is clarified that the Excused 
Contributor’s unpaid Capital Commitment will not reduce due to such 
excusal. However, in the event the Fund does not make any further 
Fund Invest ments after such excusal, the Investment Manager may, 
subject to the Regulations, cancel the unpaid Capital Commitment of 
the Excused Contributor to the extent of the quantum of excused 
investment of such Contributor.  
 
 
51 
 The Investment Manager may issue to the non -excused Contributors, 
new calls for further Capital Contributions to the extent of their 
respective unpaid Capital Commitment to cover excused or excluded 
amounts.  
 
A separate Class of Units of the Fund may be issued to Excused 
Contributors for eas e of tracking of their interest in the Fund.   
 
The Fund may make necessary adjustments at the time of making 
distribution as may be necessary or desirable to reflect the terms set out 
in this Memorandum and the Contribution Agreement with respect to 
such E xcused Contributor who has been excused from participating in 
certain Fund Investment(s) because of abovementioned reasons.

Settlor and the Trustee and any of its respective officers, directors, 
employees and agents, (ii) members of the Investment Committee, and 
any other board or committee of the Investment Manager contemplated 
in the Trust  Docum ents or any other party as may be decided by the 
Investment Manager in consultation with the members of such board or 
committee (“ Indemnified Persons ”) against any and all claims, losses, 
liabilities including Tax liabilities, costs, damages, expenses incl uding 
legal fees, and amounts paid as settlement claim incurred by them by 
reason of their association with the Scheme (“ Losses ”) except to the 
extent such Losses resulted from the Indemnified Person’s 
Malfeasance.  
 
The indemnification obligations arising under this paragraph shall be 
met out of the Contribution Fund and/or out of the distributions in 
accordance with paragraph 31.  
 
The Investment Manager may have the Scheme purchase, at the 
Scheme’s expense (as part of Operating Expenses), insurance to cove r 
the Investment Manager/ Sponsor and/or any other Indemnified 
Persons.  
 
Refer to paragraph D (9) titled  ‘Risk associated with indemnity and tax 
obligation of the Investors towards Scheme parties ’ under “ SECTION 
X: RISK FACTORS ”. 
 
17.  DEFAULTING 
CONTRIBUTORS  Payment of Capital Contributions when due is of essence , and any 
default by a Contributor in the payment thereof would cause injury to 
the Scheme and to the other non -defaulting Contributors. The default 
by a Defaulting Contributor (as defined below) may result in the loss of 
 
52 
 rights and privileges linked to th e Units held by such Contributor in the 
Scheme. Any penalty levied on the Defaulting Contributor (as 
envisaged below) shall be the amount of liquidated damages payable to 
the Scheme by the Defaulting Contributor as a consequence of default 
of his contractu al obligations.  
 
Any Contributor failing to contribute any portion of its Capital 
Commitment pursuant to a Drawdown Notice, within 21 (twenty -one) 
Business Days from the date of Drawdown Notice, may be declared as 
a defaulting Contributor (“ Defaulting Contributor ”) by the 
Investment Manager.  
 
The Investment Manager shall, at its absolute discretion, be entitled to 
enforce against the Defaulting Contributor inter alia  singly, or in 
combination, any of the action/s as stated below:  
 
 reduce the Capital Com mitments of the Defaulting Contributor to 
the amount of Capital Contributions made by such Defaulting 
Contributor;  and/or  
 
 enforce the Defaulting Contributor’s obligations through pursuing 
any rights and remedies the Scheme may have against the 
Defaulting Contributor, including by taking legal or other action 
against the Defaulting Contributor as more fully set out in the 
Contribution Agreement and the Indenture; and/or  
 
 suspend the right of the Defaulting Contributor to participate in any 
subsequent Portfo lio Investment, subject to Applicable Laws, till 
the time the default is cured to the satisfaction of the Investment 
Manager; and/or  
 
 suspend or terminate the Defaulting Contributor’s right to receive 
any distributions. However, the Defaulting Contributor shall 
remain fully liable to the creditors and liabilities of the Scheme 
(including tax liabilities), to the extent permitted by Applicable 
Law, for the amount payable by the Contributor as if such default 
had not occurred; and/or  
 
 suspend or terminate the  Defaulting Contributor’s obligation and 
right to make future payments towards its Capital Commitment; 
and/or  
 
 following the date of default, not allocate any amounts to the 
Defaulting Contributor; and/or  
 
53 
  
 cease to share any information or reports relating  to the Scheme 
with the Defaulting Contributor till the default is cured; and/or  
 
 require additional contribution (over and above the Capital 
Commitment) calculated by applying a penal interest rate of up to 
18% (eighteen percent) per annum, on the Capital  Contribution 
mentioned in the Drawdown Notice, from the date of the 
Drawdown Notice to the date of actual remittance of the amount 
(such defaulted Capital Contribution plus additional contribution 
in accordance with this Clause being the “ Default Amount ”); 
and/or  
 
 forfeit, without compensation, some or all Units subscribed by the 
Defaulting Contributor. Upon such forfeiture being effected, the 
Defaulting Contributor shall cease to be entitled to any rights 
including the right to demand refund of its forfei ted portion of the 
Capital Contribution to the Contribution Fund; and/or  
 
 prohibit the Defaulting Contributor from participating in any 
subsequent Contributor vote, meeting, consent or decision to be 
made by the Scheme or Investment Committee or any other 
committee of Investment Manager (as applicable); and/or  
 
 sell the Defaulting Contributor’s Units to other non -defaulting 
Investors and / or to third parties at a price as solely decided by the 
Investment Manager; and/or  
 
 offset amounts otherwise distributable to such Defaulting 
Contributor against the Default Amount and Scheme Expenses; 
and/or  
 
 recover the following from the Defaulting Contributor, which if 
not paid, will give rise to a new default, or set off the same against 
sums otherwise distr ibutable to the Defaulting Contributors: (a) all 
due Operating Expenses; (b) due Management Fee; (c) any cost 
incurred by the Scheme as a result of taking actions against the 
Defaulting Contributor; and (d) any other amount for which it is 
liable to pay to  the Scheme as per Applicable Laws and under the 
Contribution Agreement; and (e) indemnification or any other 
obligations under the Contribution Agreement.  
 
Any or all of  the above actions subject to the Applicable Laws may be 
waived by the Investment Manager. Any Taxes arising as a 
 
54 
 consequence of such actions shall be separately recoverable from the 
Defaulting Contributor.  
 
Defaulting Contributor shall not be allowed to p articipate in subsequent 
Scheme Investment(s), until the default is cured by such Contributor. 
Notwithstanding the above, a Defaulting Contributor shall not be 
allowed by the Scheme/Investment Manager to participate in any 
subsequent Scheme Investment till  the time the Defaulting Contributor 
makes payment of Default Amount to the Scheme and cures the default 
to the satisfaction of the Investment Manager.  
 
In the event the Units of a Defaulting Contributor are forfeited, such 
forfeiture shall include all div idends, interest, gains and distributions 
declared but unpaid and all payments made by the Contributor in 
respect of such Units. Any Units so forfeited may be disposed -off by 
the Investment Manager, and the proceeds of disposal (if any) shall 
accrue to the  Scheme. Upon such forfeiture being effected, the 
Defaulting Contributor shall cease to be entitled to any rights (including 
the right to demand refund) in respect of its contribution to the Scheme. 
The Units not so forfeited may be redeemed at such price as may be 
determined by the Investment Manager and shall be calculated after 
taking into account any expenses (including Management Fees that may 
accrue), deductions, losses allocated to such Defaulting Contributor.  
 
Notwithstanding  the above, the Investme nt Manager may, at its sole 
discretion and based on the needs of the Scheme, allow Capital 
Contributions already made by the Defaulting Contributor to remain 
invested and to be returned to the Defaulting Contributor at the time of 
disposal of the Scheme’s investments in Portfolio Companies, together 
with distributions to other Contributors.  
 
In the event of any forfeiture of Units of a Defaulting Contributor, the 
pro rata  share of the other non -defaulting Contributors shall get 
automatically realigned based  on such reduction in the Corpus of the 
Scheme.  
 
Any additional amounts collected from the Defaulting Contributor 
and/or in respect of sale/transfer of Units of such Defaulting 
Contributor, shall be retained by the Investment Manager as part of the 
Contri bution Fund to be utilized towards satisfaction of Scheme  
Expenses and the balance, if any, shall be distributed upon termination 
of the Scheme or in the alternative shall be immediately distributed 
amongst the existing non -defaulting Contributors in propo rtion to their 
 
55 
 respective Capital Contributions and shall be subject to recall by the 
Investment Manager.  
 
Notwithstanding the above actions taken by the Investment Manager, 
the Defaulting Contributor will remain liable to pay to the Scheme (as 
applicable) : 
(a) Default Amount;  
(b) any amounts for which it is liable to pay to the Scheme under 
the Indenture and/or the Contribution Agreement; and  
(c)  any Tax liabilities – any Taxes in relation to the aforesaid 
actions shall be borne by the Defaulting Contributor  
 
The Investment Manager may take different action/s  against different 
Defaulting Contributors and shall not in any way be liable to any 
Contributor or other person for any such actions taken or for any 
inconsistency in the application of any actions followi ng a default by a 
Defaulting Contributor.  
 
In the event there is any change in Applicable Law, due to which the 
Contributor is restricted from making any further Capital Contributions 
to the Scheme, then, subject to the Regulations, the Contributor shall 
not be obliged to make any further Capital C ontributions to the Scheme 
and have the right to review its Capital Commitment, without being 
termed as a Defaulting Contributor or incurring any penalty for such  
action under the Trust Documents.   
 
Provided that nothing in this paragraph 15 shall apply in  the event the 
Contributor does not make any Capital Contributions  as a result of (i) 
the Investment Manager being in material breach of its material 
obligations under the Trust Documents  for which a notice in writing 
(including by electronic form. The sam e shall apply hereinafter this 
paragraph) has been served to the Investment Manager, (through the 
Trustee) specifying the nature of breach  ; (ii) the Investment Manager 
being  in the process of being  removed pursuant to paragraph 33 below ; 
or (iii) the Cause has occurred and is continuing.  For the avoidance of 
doubt, the Contributor shall make Capital Contributions pursuant to  the 
Drawdown Notice  as long as such Notice has been provided  to the 
Contributor after the Investment Manager  has provide d written 
confirmation  to the Contributor that the event described  in (i) above  has 
been cured  and the same has been determined by the  Contributor  to its 
satisfaction .

 TRANSMISSION OF

Investment Manager which may be denied by the Investment Manager, 
and shall be subject to the following requirements:  
 
a) The propos ed transferee/pledgee is an Eligible Person;

c) The p roposed transfer/pledge will not contravene any Applicable

In the event of transfer of Units by a Contributor, the new contributor 
shall execute a deed of adherence acknowledging to be bound by the

Laws, costs and duties with respect to such deed of adherence shall be

transferor/transferee.  
 
Notwithstanding the above, in the event of  (i) the Investment Manager 
being in the process of being removed pursuant to paragraph 33 below  
or (ii) the Cause has occurred and is continuing , the Contributors shall 
be entitled to transfer their Units to any Eligible Person without the 
prior consent of the Investment Manager  and subject to  signing of the 
relevant documents as prescribed by the Investment Manager and 
Applicable Laws . The Investment Manager shall take all necessary 
actions required under Applicable Law s to ensure the completion of 
such transfer.   
 
Redemption of Units  
 
The Investment Manager will endeavor to exit/liquidate all the Scheme 
Investments before the expiry of Term  (except in cases of in-specie  
distribution or as otherwise provided under the Regulations), and the 
liquidation proceeds along with other distributable income or assets of 
the Scheme will be distributed, subject to applicable withholding Taxes, 
as detailed under paragraph 25 under the  term ‘ Distributions ’ in this 
 
57 
 “SECTION VII: PRINCIPAL TERMS OF THE SCHEME ”. The 
Investment Manager may redeem such part of the Units as the 
Investment Manager decides for the purpose of effecting a distribution 
or otherwise. Any Tax liability or obligation  arising from such 
redemption shall be sole responsibility of the Contributor. Post the 
redemption of Unit/s and payment of consideration, the Contributor 
shall cease to be entitled to any rights in respect thereof and accordingly 
its name shall be removed  . Units not redeemed by the Scheme shall be 
redeemed as per the Applicable Laws after the expiry of the Term.  
 
Deceased Investor  
 
In the event of the death of an Investor (“ Deceased Investor ”), the 
Investment Manager, may in its discretion, take any actio n in respect of 
the Capital Commitment and/or Units of such Deceased Investor on 
equitable grounds subject to compliance with the procedural 
requirements of the Investment Manager including execution of such 
necessary documentation as may be prescribed by the Investment 
Manager. The legal heir of the Deceased Investor, or in case a nominee 
has been notified by the Deceased Investor to the Investment Manager 
before his/her death then such nominee, shall be deemed to be the 
successor of the Deceased Investor,  subject to compliance under the 
Applicable Laws and completion of procedural requirements of the 
Investment Manager including execution of such necessary 
documentation as may be prescribed by the Investment Manager. The 
actions that the Investment Manager  may take shall include but not be 
limited to permitting the legal heir / successor of the Deceased Investor 
to substitute the Deceased Investor in the Scheme by transmission of 
the Units to the legal heir / successor and/or such legal heir / successor 
agreeing to honour Capital Commitment of such Deceased Contributor; 
providing an exit in respect of the Units of such Deceased Investor; 
exempting the Capital Commitment of the Deceased Investor from any 
penal actions due to default on such Capital Commitment , etc.  
 
It is hereby clarified that in the case of joint Investors, the surviving 
Investor shall be deemed to be the sole Investor on the demise of 
another Investor.  
 
Further, it is hereby clarified that any actions by the Investment 
Manager as stated abov e on death of a Contributor, shall constitute full 
and valid discharge of the Trustee and/or the Investment Manager 
and/or the Scheme of any liability towards the legal heirs of the 
Deceased Investor.

 19.  MANAGEMENT 
FEE As a consideration for the services to  be rendered by the Investment 
Manager, the Scheme shall pay management fee to the Investment 
Manager (“ Management Fees ”). 
 
The said Management  Fee shall accrue to the Investment Manager on 
a monthly basis  and be payable by the holders of  Class A Units, C lass 
B Units, Class C Units , Class D Units  at the  rate of up to 2% (two 
percent) p.a. on such portion of Capital Contribution  received  on the 
Drawdown  Date by the respective  Contributor.   
 
The Investment Manager, in its sole discretion, may waive or reduce  
the Management Fees for a particular Contributor.  
The Management Fee payable to the Investment Manager shall be 
exclusive of all applicable Taxes (including GST) and levies, if any 
(together with surcharge and additional surcharge, as may be 
applicable) l eviable on such Management Fee, the same to be borne by 
the Trust and allocated solely to the holders of Class A Units, Class B 
Units, Class C Units and Class D Units, as applicable.

to time, and any out -of-pocket expenses as may be incurred by the 
Trustee.
